Code: commit 3f06d7840ebb42090feeb4332608a0b9d5451ee0, uncommitted changes: True | seed 7, calendars from 2026-10-28

HOW TO USE THIS FILE
  Read it top to bottom, or paste or upload it into any AI chat and ask questions, for example:
    - What is group 2026-10-31/g1, and does it make sense for these people?
    - Who said what to whom about it, and why did each person's agent answer the way it did?
    - What is e117's personality, and did their agent stay true to it all run?
    - Was anyone placed into something their profile rules out? Who was left out, and why?
    - Did the hub claim things about people that their profiles don't support?
  Ask the AI to cite event numbers like [#560]: every one points to the verbatim record in
  full_simulation.jsonl and full_transcript.txt in this run's folder.

WHAT THE WORDS MEAN
  hub          the Clawnly matchmaker (orchestrator), claude-opus-5-5. It plans groups and talks to each agent one to one.
  agent        one emulated person's own AI, claude-sonnet-5. It knows only that person's profile and answers for them.
               Agents never talk to each other. Each agent call is a fresh conversation: no memory.
  break room   a pool code builds per activity and time of day: everyone free, interested, within
               budget and not avoiding anything about it. The hub may only invite from it.
  keenness     how much a person likes the activity's tags (likes are weighted 1-3).
  round 1 / 2  round 1 asks everyone the plan proposed; round 2 asks only who the hub re-asks after
               the resolve step (alternates for a no, people whose time changed).
  yes/no/counter  an agent's answer; a counter proposes one change.
  locked       code confirms a group once 2+ people said yes; dropped otherwise.
  code showed the agent   what matchmaker.plan_checks found in the profile against the plan.

SECTIONS
  1. PEOPLE: personality prompts, before and after
  2. CONVERSATIONS: every message, day by day and group by group
  3. GROUPS: each group and its logic
  4. TIMELINE: every event, one line each
  5. WHAT OUR CHECKS FOUND (read last; skip it for an independent review)

====================================================================================================
1. PEOPLE: each emulated person's personality prompt (before) and what happened to them (after)
====================================================================================================

THE AGENT PROMPT IN FULL, exactly as e001's agent got it as its system prompt.
Everyone's prompt has this exact wording; only the name and the part under 'TOLD YOU' differ.
Code checked this for every person below; anyone whose prompt differs has it printed in full.
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
    You are the personal agent of Nikhil R.. You know Nikhil only from their profile and calendar below. You speak for Nikhil to the Clawnly matchmaker. You never talk to other people's agents.
    
    WHAT NIKHIL TOLD YOU:
    Nikhil is 37, a commuter in Lake Sawyer, with kids at home.
    Loves: watch sports, golf, crafts.
    Not into: history, book club.
    Avoids: late nights, early mornings.
    Budget: free or low-cost things.
    This week: Wednesday 28 Oct: free evening; Thursday 29 Oct: busy; Friday 30 Oct: free evening; Saturday 31 Oct: free morning, afternoon, evening; Sunday 1 Nov: free evening; Monday 2 Nov: busy; Tuesday 3 Nov: busy.
    
    Check the plan against everything above before you answer: the time against their calendar and what they avoid, the activity against their likes and dislikes, the cost against their budget. Code has listed what it found; weigh it, and look for anything it missed.
    What Nikhil avoids is a limit they set, not a preference to trade off: if the plan runs into it, say no (or counter, if the time can move), however much they'd enjoy the rest.
    - "no" if something clearly conflicts and no small change fixes it, or if Nikhil just wouldn't be keen. A mild interest is a fair reason to pass. Saying no is normal and helps the matchmaker.
    - "counter" with one concrete change that would make it a yes (for example a later start inside a day part they're free). A fixed-time event can't move, so don't counter its time.
    - "yes" only if it genuinely fits and Nikhil would want to go.
    Reply with only this JSON object: {"answer": "yes" or "no" or "counter", "counter": "the change, if any", "say": "one or two sentences to the matchmaker, as Nikhil's agent"}
----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
